# Vector everywhere: deviations from the build contract (vector-everywhere-spec.md)

## 2026-11-11, VE-00 (CI lane)

- **Push to main is filtered by a scope job, not by the trigger.** The contract asks for every push to `vector-everywhere`, and for pull requests and pushes to `main` with a `paths:` list. One `on.push` block cannot give one of its branches a path filter, and a path filter on the branch would skip the lane after a rebase onto main (the old and new tips differ only in main's files). So `on.push` lists both branches with no paths, `on.pull_request` carries the `paths:` list, and a small `scope` job compares a push to main (`git diff --name-only --no-renames before after`) against that same `pull_request.paths` list, read from the workflow file with Bun's YAML parser and globs. The typecheck and test jobs wait for its answer. A change it cannot list (a new ref, missing history) runs the lane. The patterns use only `*` and `**`, which GitHub's filter and Bun's globs read the same way.
- **The paths list is wider than 6.6's wording, to cover files the WPs name.** Added `packages/web/src/pages/everywhere/**` (VE-42's `everywhere/link.astro` sits in a folder that `everywhere*.astro` does not match), `packages/web/src/components/marketing/everywhere/**` (6.6's second bullet), `packages/app/src/components/settings-v2/everywhere*` (VE-41's `everywhere-model.ts` is not `.tsx`), `packages/web/test/**/everywhere*` and `packages/schema/test/**/everywhere*` (so test helpers such as the fetch guard count too), and the two main helpers the runner imports read-only, `packages/desktop/src/main/github-auth.ts` and `github-api.ts`, beside the api/\_lib helpers the contract names.
- **The lane has a test of its own.** The contract's test for VE-00 is "the lane itself". packages/cloud/src/workflow-compliance.test.ts is on the 6.4 avoid list, so the lane's shape and its two scripts (the scope rule and the test picker) are held by a new packages/web/test/everywhere-lane.test.ts, which runs both scripts against temporary git repositories with a stand-in `bun`. The picker runs it in CI like any other everywhere test, so the lane never runs empty.
- **The lane also runs workflow-compliance.test.ts.** The test workflow checks it only on pull requests and pushes to main, so the lane runs it on pushes to the branch too (one step, no edit to that file).
- **Redis is the Valkey image the other lanes use.** The test job's service is `valkey/valkey:7.2.14-alpine`, pinned by the same digest as vector-sharing.yml, exposed on 6379 with `VECTOR_TEST_REDIS_URL=redis://127.0.0.1:6379`.
- **Steps that only some packages need run only when those packages have everywhere tests.** ripgrep (engine and core network guards), the turbo build of the engine, core and app dependencies, and the Electron runtime for desktop tests are gated with `hashFiles` on their packages' everywhere test paths.

## 2026-11-11, VE-01 (shared types and limits)

Nothing in the contract was wrong about the code. These are the choices packages/schema/src/everywhere.ts makes where the contract leaves a detail open, so later packages read it the same way.

- **Ids.** A jid is `vej_` plus 22 lowercase Crockford base32 characters: 10 of creation time in milliseconds, then 12 random ones. Ids sort by time, and every character is allowed in `vector/ve-[0-9a-z]{12}`. Which 12 (or 8) characters a branch takes is VE-02's `branchFor`. Lease ids and offer `jti`s are 26 random characters of the same alphabet. Devices use Codium Remote's device id pattern, copied, since that file is Codium's. Account ids are the UUIDs `requireAccountUser` accepts.
- **Offer times are milliseconds**, like Vector's other signed tokens (`vct_`, `vdg_`). "exp − iat ≤ 30 s" is `exp - iat <= 30_000`.
- **Codes.** 4.1 item 1 says the laptop declines a GitHub handoff whose trigger comment changed but names no code: it is `trigger-changed`, which, like `busy`, is not lasting (the server answers `handled: false`). The refusal codes add the cases 3.8 and 4.4 refuse without naming a code: `not-linked` (answered with the link prompt), `external`, `guest`, `bot`, `automation`, `not-connected`, `shared-place`, `link-invalid` and `unsupported`.
- **Strict and lenient decoders.** Everything a computer sends (report, settle, claim, decline) and the parsed `InboundTrigger` refuse unknown fields; offer claims do too. `decodeJob` drops unknown fields instead, so a server that adds an optional field in a later version does not stop older desktops; the runner only reads what the decoder returns. A job of kind `stop` or `command` is refused, though the type keeps the contract's union.
- **GitHub carries no thread text.** A GitHub trigger or job must have an empty task and context and a `github` reference (4.1); other platforms may not carry a `github` reference, and only Linear and Jira may carry an `issue`.
- **Settle summaries** are also refused when they hold a fenced code block, beside the diff forms E4 names; `sanitizeReply` on the computer already refuses fences, so a real summary never has one.
- **Thread links** must be https on the platform's own host: github.com, slack.com and its subdomains, linear.app, and subdomains of atlassian.net. VE-13 widens Jira's if its re-read finds other site hosts.
- **Slack installation keys** take three forms: `slack:E…:T…` (a workspace in a Grid organisation), `slack:T…` (a workspace on its own) and `slack:E…` (an organisation-wide install). VE-11 picks which it writes.
- **A job's reply format** must be its platform's: Slack mrkdwn for Slack, Markdown for GitHub and Linear, plain text for Jira.

## 2026-11-11, VE-02 (pure policy and framing)

Nothing here contradicts the contract's intent. These are the places packages/schema/src/everywhere-policy.ts, packages/core/src/everywhere/frame.ts and publish.ts differ from the contract's wording or fill a gap it leaves, so later packages read them the same way.

- **`decideRoute` runs twice.** It answers `offer` when the laptop is eligible; the intake offers, waits up to 20 s and calls it again with the same facts and `offer: {claimed, declines, unanswered}`. When every computer declined for a lasting reason but the reasons differ, the refusal names one in the order `not-mapped`, `model-not-allowed`, `local-mode`, `off` (the computer closest to working).
- **GitHub handoffs hand back.** For platform `github` every outcome but a claim is `{action: "hand-back", code}`, which VE-10 answers as `handled: false`: the repository's own Actions run is the fallback (4.1), so Vector everywhere never queues, dispatches or posts a refusal for a GitHub mention. Identity there is the trigger comment's author id against the binder's id (`mayTrigger` row `github-laptop-first`).
- **The queue needs what the laptop needs, except being online now.** Step 7 lists only "connected in the last 30 days" and "Wait for my computer". A queued job ends up on the computer, so it also needs the requester allowed on that computer (E14), the `remote` perk and Remote configured. A refusal says which (`no-place` with `why.computer` of `not-seen`, `wait-off`, `not-allowed` or `no-remote`).
- **`no-place` names both routes.** `why.actions` is `none` (no Actions route yet: no port, source not enabled, no R-A4 or R-A9), `bind` (the route exists but the repository is not bound to the sponsor, the App is missing or the workflow is not in Codium mode) or `public`. Step 6 asks for a private repository, so `internal` and a failed read count as not private.
- **Replies are sanitized to plain Markdown, then written per platform.** `sanitizeReply` returns platform-neutral Markdown and `formatText` writes it as Slack mrkdwn (escaped), Markdown or Jira plain text. Sanitizing twice changes nothing, so the computer (before settle) and the server (in `renderResult`) both run it without double escaping.
- **Stricter or wider than the wording, on purpose.** Images are removed whatever their host. github.com links stay only when they point into the job's repository, on every platform, GitHub included. Mentions are wrapped in code on every platform, not only GitHub. Slack `<@…>`, `<!…>`, `<#…>`, Jira `[~…]` and Linear profile links are removed. E-mail addresses and HTML comments (a forged `<!-- vector:job … -->` among them) are removed. Entities are decoded and zero-width, soft-hyphen and direction characters removed before any check, so none of them can hide a mention, a link or a token.
- **File names are code, not domains.** A bare token with a source-file extension (`README.md`, `setup.py`, `Node.js`), or one followed by `(` (`config.load()`), is kept wrapped in code instead of removed; inside code spans nothing is linkified. Every other bare domain outside the allowed hosts is removed.
- **Withheld texts.** The contract gives the posting-check text. `WITHHELD.secret` and `WITHHELD.code` are the fixed texts for a summary withheld because it looked like a secret or held code.
- **Shared places get even less.** A shared-place result carries only the outcome line and the PR link: also no branch name, held-back count, checks line or `needs` list (they go in `privateText`).
- **`branchFor(jid, issue?)` takes `{key, platform}`**, not a bare key, because the key is lowercased for Linear and kept as written for Jira. It uses the jid's random tail (the last 12, or 8, characters), so two jobs in one millisecond get different branches.
- **`heldBack`.** `.github/`, `.husky/` and `.githooks/` are held back at the top level, as their globs say. `.gitattributes` and `.gitmodules` are held back at any depth (a nested `.gitattributes` also sets filters). Paths compare case-insensitively, with `\` as `/` and Windows' trailing dots and spaces removed.
- **`stripClosingKeywords` also covers issue keys.** Beside GitHub references to other repositories, a closing word before any issue key but the job's own (`Fixes ENG-9` on job ENG-123) loses its keyword, since Linear's integration closes on those words. The word list is GitHub's plus Linear's (`closing`, `fixing`, `resolving`, `complete…`). The keyword goes and the reference stays as code. Same-repository `#12` references stay, as the contract says.
- **PR template details.** The title is the commit subject, `<issue key> Vector: <the task's first words>`, with no mention, reference, link or markup. The issue line is `Fixes ENG-123` for Linear and `PROJ-123` for Jira, as the contract says, and `Refs #12` for a GitHub job, which the contract leaves open. `commitMessage` (subject, job id, `[skip ci]`) is here too, so the runner and the template share the title.
- **Framing inputs the job does not carry.** `EverywhereJob` has no thread link and a GitHub job no thread text, so `frameTask(job, {thread?, threadUrl?})` takes them. `thread` is required for GitHub (labels `writer` or `untrusted`) and refused elsewhere. A GitHub link comes from the job's reference. Other links are written only when `threadUrlAllowed` accepts them. The thread the computer read must fit the same limits as a job's context (VE-10 trims it).
- **Small additions VE-05 to VE-07 need, all pure.** `renderEnd` (`lost`, `expired`, `not-started`, and Jira's two 50-minute texts), `ROUTE_NOTES` for `renderMilestone` (`received` "On it.", `finding`, `laptop`, `actions`, `waiting`), `overLimit`, `queueDeadline`, `SECRET_PATTERNS` (for the desktop's staged-diff scan), and in frame.ts `OUTSIDE_KEY`, `UNATTENDED_KEY`, `unattendedMetadata`, `outsideMetadata` and `sessionTitle`. frame.ts keeps its own copy of the outside key, so it does not load Brain. Its test checks that the copy equals `BrainSchema.OUTSIDE`.

## 2026-11-11, VE-01 and VE-02 review fixes (phase P1a)

These change choices recorded above; where they differ, this entry wins.

- **The queue also needs the `remote` switch on.** Nothing can be offered through a relay that is switched off, so `decideRoute` refuses `no-place` instead of queueing, with a new `why.computer` of `remote-off` ("Vector Remote is paused, so no computer can take jobs right now."). `no-remote` still means no perk or Remote not configured, and is named first.
- **A claim is final.** On the second call `decideRoute` answers `laptop` for `offer.claimed` before identity, plan, caps, repository and laptop eligibility, since the jobs route already moved the job to `claimed`. Only the global switch at `stop` still refuses, as the claim route does; `no-new`, the platform switch, the `remote` switch, a lapsed heartbeat and a cap counter that now counts the job do not. `RouteInput.counts` holds the counts from before the job.
- **`sanitizeReply` cleans until the text stops changing.** Removing markup can join what it separated (a token split by `<b></b>`, a tag built by removing an empty link, a mention after a removed domain), so each pass runs again, at most 8 times (text still changing after that is withheld with `WITHHELD.code`), and the secret and code checks run on the result as well as the input. That, not a single pass, is why sanitizing twice changes nothing.
- **Entities, escapes and code spans read as a renderer reads them.** Entities are decoded until none is left, with invisible characters removed every round; text still decoding after 32 rounds loses every `&`. Code spans are rewritten to one backtick each side on one line (backslashes before a backtick and unpaired backticks go, touching spans get a space), and outside code a backslash before ASCII punctuation goes. This also closes two mention forms the review did not list: `\@name`, and a mention after two backticks that close nothing, both of which render as plain text.
- **Address shapes.** A scheme with one slash, a backslash or none (`https:/host`, `http:host`) and a scheme-relative `//host` or `\\host` count as links. A bare host counts after any character but a letter, digit, `_` or `@`, with at most 126 labels so long runs of dots stay linear. A dot-led name after a space (`.env.local`) is kept as code, and a relative path to a source file (`src/utils.ts`, `../a.md`) becomes one code span. Inside code, `//…` is left alone (code shows as written, so `//go:build` stays); scheme and `www.` addresses still go.
- **Definitions and images.** Link reference definitions go also behind blockquote and list markers, and shortcut and collapsed reference images (`![a]`, `![a][]`) go.
- **HTML.** Only tag-shaped text on one line is removed (`<name` followed by a space, a line end, `/`, `>` or the end), so `i<len`, `Map<string, User>` and later paragraphs survive. A tag with no attributes inside code stays (`Promise<void>`); every other tag goes, inside code too.
- **Mentions** count after `.`, `/`, `-` and `+` too, as GitHub reads them; `wrapMentions` leaves e-mail addresses alone.
- **`looksLikeCode`** treats CR, U+0085, U+2028 and U+2029 as line ends, ignores invisible format and control characters, and allows leading spaces, so the settle decoder refuses those diffs too (E4).
- **The result's checks line** speaks of the pull request only when there is a link, of the branch when a pushed branch has no PR, and is left out for a `held` result.
- **`stripClosingKeywords` keeps the words.** The reference becomes code and the closing word stays (``Fixes `ENG-9` ``), so `Fixed utf-8 decoding` keeps its sentence. Keys still match in any case rather than only uppercase, as the review suggested, so a lowercase key cannot slip through; a term that only looks like a key is shown as code.
- **Not done: a secret scan in `decodeSettle`.** The review offered it as optional. `decodeSettle` lives in everywhere.ts, which the policy module imports, and the server already sanitizes the summary again in `renderResult`.

## 2026-11-11, VE-03 (server foundation)

Nothing in the contract was wrong about the code except one type rule (the last item). These are the choices api/\_lib/everywhere/{config,keys,store,crypto,switch,audit,dedupe,signature,limits,after,fetch}.ts make where the contract leaves a detail open, so later packages read them the same way.

- **The data key and sealing.** `VECTOR_EVERYWHERE_DATA_KEY` must decode to exactly 32 bytes (standard or URL-safe base64, padded or not). A shorter, longer or malformed value, or a placeholder whose bytes barely vary (fewer than 8 distinct values, such as all zeros), turns the feature off. The key id is the first 12 base64url characters of a SHA-256 of the key, so no extra variable is needed, and a later rotation only adds a second key to the ring (`open` already takes a ring; `seal` uses its first key). A sealed value is `ves1.<key id>.<iv>.<ciphertext and tag>`, all base64url, and is bound to a context (its store key) passed as associated data, so a sealed token copied into another record does not open there. `open` tells `unknown-key` from `tampered` and `malformed`, and refuses non-canonical base64url.
- **Env reading.** `VECTOR_EVERYWHERE_ENABLED` is trimmed before it is compared with `true` (env editors add line ends). A secret shorter than 16 characters or with spaces in it counts as missing, which turns its platform off; the HMAC helpers also refuse such a secret themselves. Ids (`VECTOR_SLACK_APP_ID`, client ids, `VECTOR_JIRA_FORGE_APP_ID`) must be printable with no spaces, at most 200 characters. GitHub needs none of our secrets (the handoff's OIDC verifier is injected, VE-10 and VE-31), so listing it is enough. The offer key must be an Ed25519 PKCS#8 private key (`\n` escapes accepted) with a key id matching `OFFER_KEY_ID_PATTERN`; without both there are no offers, but the rest of the feature can run. `readConfig` also answers `problems`: the names of variables that are set but unusable or needed and missing, never their values. An explicit `false` is not a problem.
- **The switch.** The environment gives only `on` or `stop`; `no-new` comes from Codium's switch once R-C6 adds it. The seam calls Codium's `readSwitch` only when Codium's `SWITCH_NAMES` lists `everywhere`, so it starts working with no change here. Codium's value can only make the switch stricter; Codium's `off`, an unknown value and an unreadable store all read `stop`. Codium's `remote` switch reads `on` or `stop` the same way, and `stop` when it cannot be read. `admits(state, "new" | "running")` is the one rule: `new` is a new trigger at the intake; `running` covers a claim of an offered or waiting job, reports, heartbeats, settles, the dispatch of an accepted job and the re-offer of a waiting one, which matches the phase P1a rule that a claim is final.
  *2026-11-15 note (EV-07):* Codium's SWITCH_NAMES now lists `everywhere` with `on`, `no-new` and `stop` (R-C6), so the seam reads Codium's own record; switch.ts changed in comments only. See "2026-11-15, EV-07".
- **The store guard.** `everywhereStore` refuses, before `persistentStore` sees it, any command outside a fixed list or touching a key that is not under `vector:everywhere:`, so this code can never read or write Codium's or anyone else's records. Every write names its TTL except the records 3.13 keeps until changed.
- **Keys.** Every part is checked against its id's shape and account ids are written lowercase. Ids whose shape a platform or peer chooses (delivery ids, Forge token ids, A2A task ids) are kept when they match `[A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,128}` and otherwise replaced by `~` and their SHA-256, which no plain id contains. Platform user ids have one pattern per platform (Slack `[A-Z0-9]`, Linear UUIDs, Jira account ids with at most one colon, GitHub numbers), so `link:<installation>:<user>` stays unambiguous.
- **The audit log is a sorted set by time**, not a list, so entries older than 90 days can be dropped by age. One Lua script adds the entry, drops entries older than 90 days, keeps the newest 1,000 and moves the log's expiry. An entry has exactly `id`, `at`, `event`, `platform`, `installation`, `jid`, `code`, `device`, `actor` and `userId`; any other field is refused. `code` comes from a closed list (refusal, decline, outcome, route, job state and ending codes, erase reasons, and `admin` for a re-install by a platform admin), so no free text can travel in it. Events add `uninstall` and `disconnect` to the contract's list.
- **Cap counters.** Day and hour windows are UTC calendar windows (`d20261111`, `h2026111114`), and each counter expires a minute after its window ends. Live jobs are the size of `jobs:<sponsor>`, which VE-05 keeps. `readCounts` gives `decideRoute` the counts from before the job; `countJob` checks every cap again and counts the job in one Lua script, so two jobs arriving together cannot both take the last place.
- **Dedupe.** A delivery whose work never finished is not released by a call: its `pending` mark simply expires after 60 seconds, in time for the platform's retry. `useOnce(store, key, ttl)` is the same `SET NX` for single-use ids (Forge token ids, offer ids, GitHub handoffs).
- **`after()`.** "Runs inline where none exists" is read as: without a Vercel request context the work still starts at once in this process. `after` answers a promise that never rejects, so a caller can await it, and `drainAfter()` waits for every running step, for tests and the sweep. A failure is logged with the step's fixed name and an error code only.
- **`platformFetch` also checks paths.** Beside the host list, each host has its allowed paths: `slack.com` `/api/`; `api.linear.app` `/graphql` and `/oauth/`; `api.github.com` any path; `api.atlassian.com` `/ex/jira/` (the Forge remote's API base URL); and `forge.cdn.prod.atlassian-dev.net` `/.well-known/jwks.json` only (the Forge Invocation Token keys, which VE-13's fit.ts pins). Paths with encoded dots or slashes are refused. Only https on the default port with no user name or password; redirects are always errors, even when a caller asks to follow them; calls time out after 10 seconds unless the caller passes a signal. `readPlatformJson` reads at most 1 MiB. VE-11 to VE-13 re-check these hosts and paths against the platform pages.
- **Tests beyond the contract's list.** packages/web/test/everywhere-{keys,limits,after}.test.ts, and two helpers, packages/web/test/lib/everywhere-fetch-guard.ts (fails the test on any request off 127.0.0.1, localhost or [::1]) and packages/web/test/lib/everywhere-store.ts (an in-memory store behind the Redis REST API that knows the everywhere scripts by their exact text, and a bridge to the CI Redis). The lane's `packages/web/test/**/everywhere*` path covers both. Cases needing a real Redis (concurrent claims of a delivery and a single-use id, the audit and count scripts) skip locally without `VECTOR_TEST_REDIS_URL`.
- **A type fix in VE-02's policy module.** The root tsconfig that checks api/ turns on `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`, which the schema package's tsconfig turns off, so importing everywhere-policy.ts from api/ failed the api typecheck in `stripTags`. `codeRanges` now declares its tuple return type. No behaviour changes; packages/schema/test/everywhere-policy.test.ts passes as before.

## 2026-11-11, VE-04 (identity, links, installs and mappings)

Nothing in the contract was wrong about the code. These are the choices api/\_lib/everywhere/identity.ts, erase.ts and account-api.ts and the routes api/everywhere/{link,links,installs,repos,oauth-finish}.ts make where the contract leaves a detail open.

- **Files beyond the list.** api/\_lib/everywhere/erase.ts (the erase module R-C9 names), api/\_lib/everywhere/account-api.ts (the five handlers, built from injected dependencies; each route file is one line), and packages/web/test/lib/everywhere-account.ts (an account service fake, a loopback route harness and a Jira permission fake, all on 127.0.0.1). The VE-03 fake store gains sets, `GETDEL` and the two new scripts. store.ts gains `takeValue`, `takeJson`, `setMembers`, `addMembers` and `removeMembers`, and `swapValue` can also delete.
- **Keys beyond 3.13.** `linked:<installation>` (user ids linked there), `repos:<installation>` (mapped places), `linkcodes:<account>` (the hash of the account's one live code) and `linktries:<installation>:<user>` (failed attempts, one hour). Each index set is written before its record and an entry whose record is gone or points elsewhere is ignored, so erasing finds every record without scanning the store.
- **"A session whose platform identity differs."** A link code is made for one platform (required) and, when the page names it, one installation. Redeemed from another platform or installation it is refused (`link-invalid`) and used up. For Linear the OAuth state plays the code's part (single use, 10 minutes) and the sign-in must finish from the account that started it. A code forwarded to another person on the same platform still links, because both proofs pass; the defence there is the contract's notices: the platform user's private notice names the account's masked e-mail, and the account gets an e-mail and an audit entry, which the page shows with an unlink button. The tests say so.
- **Codes.** Stored as an HMAC-SHA256 under the data key, not a plain SHA-256, because an 8-letter code has only 40 bits. The alphabet is the digits and capitals without I, L, O and U; a typed O, I or L reads as 0, 1 or 1; codes show as groups of four with hyphens. Connect codes have 12 letters. Only an account's newest link code works, so making one ends the older. Every attempt that names a live code uses it up, whatever the answer. After 10 failed attempts in an hour a platform user is refused before any code is read.
- **The link notice.** The e-mail goes through the site's existing mail sender (`RESEND_API_KEY`, `VECTOR_PURCHASE_EMAIL_FROM`) when both are set; otherwise the audit entry is the only notice. It names the platform, the user id and the installation, never a display name. The account's e-mail is sealed into the code record for the code's 10 minutes and kept nowhere after. A failed send is logged by code and never undoes the link.
- **Moving a link.** A platform user who redeems another account's code moves the link, and the old account's log gets `unlink` with code `unlinked`. Linking again to the same account changes nothing and sends no second notice.
- **Installs.** The OAuth state may name the installation the install started for; an exchange that lands elsewhere is refused (`EVERYWHERE_WRONG_PLACE`, 4.2). The platforms' exchanges (Slack's install at VE-11, Linear's install and sign-in at VE-12) plug into `createAccountApi({exchanges})`; without one the finish answers 501. The authorize URLs and scopes are in `OAUTH` for VE-11 and VE-12 to re-check against the platform pages. A Jira connect code's issuer passed the ADMINISTER check, so redeeming one for a site another account connected changes the connector, as an admin's re-install does. The connect code and install records may carry the Forge installation id, for VE-13's token check.
- **Mappings.** (The `repo` word was narrowed by the phase P1b review entry below.) The record keeps 3.13's field names, with `null` in `repositoryIds` for an id not known yet: the `repo owner/name` word gives only a name, and VE-11 resolves the id. At most 20 repositories, each name once. The word is accepted from any linked person (not a bot, automation, external user or guest) on a connected installation, because at launch the sponsor is the requester's own linked account; the place keeps its private mark.
- **Routes.** While the feature is off every route answers 404 before the sign-in check and before any store read. Issuing a code, starting an install and finishing one also need the platform on. A link, installation or mapping that is not the caller's answers 404 rather than 403, so no route says whether someone else's exists. Bodies go through `requireTrustedJsonRequest` (origin and content type) and are at most 8,000 bytes. No route returns a token, a sealed value or a code's hash.
- **Erase.** (The order was changed by the phase P1b review entry below: the record goes last.) The installation record goes first (nothing new links to it), then live jobs, links (each linked account's log gets `unlink` with the reason, except an account being deleted) and mappings. The connector's log gets `uninstall` for `uninstalled`, `tokens-revoked` and `revoked`, and `disconnect` for `disconnected`. Live jobs are erased through the `JobEraser` seam, which VE-05 passes to `createAccountApi` and `EVERYWHERE_ERASE_PART`; until then there are no jobs. Account erase also deletes `jobs:<account>`, `seen:<account>` and the audit log. Revoking a platform token on disconnect is an optional `revoke` hook for the adapters (VE-06, VE-11); erase needs no data key.

## 2026-11-11, VE-05 (jobs, offers and leases)

Nothing in the contract was wrong about the code. These are the choices api/\_lib/everywhere/{jobs,offer,jobs-api}.ts and the routes api/everywhere/jobs/{claim,decline,report,settle,pending}.ts make where the contract leaves a detail open, so VE-06, VE-07 and VE-10 read them the same way.

- **Files beyond the list.** api/\_lib/everywhere/jobs-api.ts holds the five handlers, built from injected dependencies (environment, store, clock, Codium's switches, event hooks); each route file is one line, as VE-04's are. The test fake store gains the two job scripts and `ZREM`, `ZSCORE` and `ZRANGEBYSCORE … LIMIT`; the route harness gains `mount` and request headers.
- **How the fence works.** A job record is JSON whose first two fields are always `version` and `state`. The server computes the next record in TypeScript from the record it read, and one `EVAL` writes it only while the stored record still starts with that version and state (read with a fixed Lua pattern, so no JSON library is needed in the script). The same script moves the job in `jobs:due`, takes it out of the live sets when it ends and deletes its body then. A change that lost the race reads again and decides again, up to 8 times, then answers 503 `EVERYWHERE_BUSY`.
- **Keys beyond 3.13.** `livejobs:<installation>` (live jobs on an installation, so erasing it needs no scan) and `history:<account>` (every job the account sponsored in the last 30 days, for the job list and for account erase). `seen:<account>` holds `{at, device}` and `ready:<device>` holds `{account, at, …booleans}`, so the router reads "Wait for my computer" from the last computer seen.
- **Edges the diagram does not draw.** (The release after a claim was replaced by the phase P1b review entry below: a decline after a claim always refuses.) A decline after a claim (with its lease) refuses the job when the reason is lasting, and otherwise releases it: `claimed → offered`, so the intake (still in its window) or the sweep moves it on. A late claim of a job that just went back to the queue is allowed (`waiting → claimed`). Safety nets for a dead intake: `received` for 2 minutes ends `expired`, and an `offered` job past its 30-second offer goes back to `waiting` when it has a queue (`queueUntil`, kept on the record while claimed so a release can return to it), otherwise `expired`. An Actions run still `running` 135 minutes after it started (@vector's longest run of 120 minutes plus its publish step) ends `failed` until VE-30 settles it from `listCloudRuns`. A laptop job past its deadline is answered `stop`.
- **Endings.** A waiting Jira job ends with Jira's own text (`jira-waiting`), others with `expired`; `lost` and `not-started` as the contract says. Endings are audited as `settle` with the job's end state as the code.
- **The device on report, settle and pending** comes from the header `x-vector-device`, because Report and Settle refuse every field outside their shapes. A report or settle whose header or lease differs from the claim's is 409 `LEASE_MISMATCH`; a missing or malformed header is 400.
- **Answers.** claim: 200 `{job}`, or 409 `JOB_TAKEN`, `JOB_EXPIRED` or `EVERYWHERE_STOPPED` (`action: "stop"`). report and heartbeat: 200 `{action: "continue", leaseExpiresAt}`, `{action: "stop"}` (switch, cancelled job, deadline) or `{action: "lost"}`; 409 `JOB_ENDED` for a job that ended otherwise. settle: 200 `{settled: true}`; the same settle again answers the same and tells nobody twice. decline: 200 `{recorded}`. pending: 200 `{offers, waiting}`.
- **Switches.** With the feature off every route checks the token's signature and then answers `stop` with no store read. Under Codium's `stop` claims are refused, reports, heartbeats, settles and declines answer `stop`, and the call sweeps the account with `stop`: waiting, received and offered jobs expire and the rest are cancelled, so a computer's next report hears `stop` and nothing is pushed. `no-new` lets every jobs call through.
- **pending.** `GET` with the readiness booleans as query flags (`jobs`, `remote`, `local`, `sandbox`, `wait`, `room`, `projects`; `wait` defaults on, the rest off) and the device header. "The project allowed for this place's repository" is not one boolean, so the computer sends `projects` (at least one project allowed) and a `not-mapped` decline says the rest. Re-offering a waiting job moves it to `offered` and answers the signed offers in the response, so a computer picks waiting jobs up over HTTPS with no relay; VE-06 may also push them through the `offer` port. A computer that does not take jobs, or has Vector Local on, is offered nothing and the job keeps waiting.
- **What the server sends a computer.** `limits` are the highest a computer's settings accept ($50, 120 minutes, the deadline 120 minutes after the claim); for Jira the deadline is the earlier of 35 minutes after the claim and 50 minutes after intake. `reply.maxChars` is the summary limit, 1,500. The requester's sanitized display name lives in the sealed body (2 hours), not the 30-day record, which keeps ids only.
- **Stricter than the wording.** A settle's `branch` must be the job's own (the rule of core everywhere/publish.ts `branchFor`, repeated here because api/ cannot import core) or it is 400. `createJob` runs the computer's own `decodeJob` on a stand-in lease, so a job a computer would refuse is never made, and it checks the live cap of 3 inside its script, so two jobs created together cannot both take the last place (limits.ts `countJob` still counts the day and hour windows first; a refusal here leaves them one higher).
- **Offers on the server.** `signOffer`, `verifyOffer` (key id, signature, audience, account, at most 30 s of life, not expired, not dated more than 30 s ahead, and the `jti` used only after everything else passed), `offerJtiStore` (`offer:<jti>`, 1 hour) and `offerPublicKey`/`offerVerifyKey` for the raw 32-byte key VE-21 pins in `OFFER_KEYS`.
- **Hooks for VE-06.** `createJobsApi({events})` calls `claimed`, `declined`, `reported`, `settled` and `ended` after the response (`after()`), with the job record; a claim that found the body gone and the lazy sweep's endings go to `ended`. The production export has no hooks yet: VE-06 builds it with the adapters' events.
- **Erase.** jobs.ts `jobEraser` is now the default `JobEraser` of the account routes (VE-04) and of `EVERYWHERE_ERASE_PART`. Erasing an installation cancels its live jobs and deletes their bodies; erasing an account also deletes every job record and body of its 30-day history and its last computer's readiness.

## 2026-11-11, VE-03 to VE-05 review fixes (phase P1b)

These change choices recorded above; where they differ, this entry wins. Two of them are places where the contract was wrong about the code (the first two items).

- **The erase part has Codium's shape (contract wrong about the code).** R-C9 writes the part as `{id: "everywhere", erase: eraseEverywhereAccount}`, but Codium's `ErasePart` (codium-erase.ts, at the contract's codium tip and on origin/codium) is `{id, configured(), requires?, erase(account, {fetcher, now, deadline?})}`, and Codium calls `configured()` before its try. `EVERYWHERE_ERASE_PART` now has `configured` (true whenever the store's URL and token are set, as `persistentStore` reads them, so an account's records are erased even after the feature was switched off) and an `erase(account, context)` that uses the deletion's fetcher and clock. erase.ts repeats Codium's context type as `CodiumEraseContext`, since this branch cannot import Codium's file. The registration line in codium-erase.ts stays Codium's (R-C9).
- **`ready:<account>:<device>` (contract wrong about the key).** 3.13 names the readiness record `ready:<device>`, but the device id is the client's own choice, so one account could write over or delete another account's record by naming its device id. The key now holds the account too; the record, its 1-day life and what reads it are unchanged.
- **A claim is final.** After a claim, a decline with the lease ends the job `refused` with the decline's reason, whatever it is, from `claimed` and from `running`. Nothing releases a job back to `offered` any more, so no other computer and no Actions run can take a job a computer held (3.4, E8); for a GitHub handoff the job's end answers `handled: false` and @vector's own run goes on. The claim drops `queueUntil` (nothing returns to the queue after a claim), and `dispatchJob` also refuses any job that carries a claim's marks (`claimedAt` or `device`). This replaces the VE-05 item that released a job after a decline that was not lasting.
- **Dispatch writes the ticket first.** `ticket:<hash>` is written before the `dispatched` transition, so a dispatch that went through is always found by the start hook. A ticket left by a dispatch that lost starts nothing, because `startDispatched` requires the job to be `dispatched` with that very ticket.
- **Audit appends after a committed change are best effort.** Every jobs.ts audit entry that follows a written transition (create, offer, queue, dispatch, claim, decline, settle, refuse, cancel, the sweep's endings) is caught and logged by step, job id and error code. Otherwise a failed append answered 503 after the change was written, and the retry found the job already moved, so the result, the progress or the fixed reply was never posted.
- **One bad record stalls nothing.** The sweep (`sweepDue`, `sweepAccount`) and `offerWaiting` handle each job on its own and log a failure by job id and code. A job whose record is gone (expired before any sweep, or erased) leaves the index being swept, and `jobs:due` too, and its body goes, so it no longer comes back or holds a place under the live cap. A record that does not decode keeps its place: it may be a newer deployment's live job after a rollback, and dropping it from `jobs:due` would strand it once the newer code is back. Erasing deletes such a record: an installation erase deletes it from its live index, and an account erase deletes it with the history. Not done: `decodeJobRecord` still refuses unknown fields rather than dropping them, because this code writes back the record it decoded, and that would remove a newer deployment's fields.
- **The `repo` word is Slack's only, and only for an allowed requester.** `setMappingByWord` refuses Linear and Jira installations (`wrong-platform`), where the connector sets mappings on vectordev.ai. The caller passes `maySponsor` (the intake's perk check, or `sponsorListed` before the perk exists), and a linked account that may not sponsor runs is refused `not-allowed`. A mapping the connector set changes only by the connector's own word (`not-connector` for anyone else). A mapping another requester set may still be changed, as 3.8's "or an allowed requester" says. This replaces the VE-04 item that accepted the word from any linked person.
- **Erasing an installation removes its record last.** Live jobs, links and mappings go first, then the record with its place in the connector's list, then one more pass over jobs, links and mappings for anything written by a call that passed its installation check just before the record went. If a step fails, the record stays, so the disconnect (which still finds its connector), the platform's retry or the account deletion's next pass runs the erase again. This replaces the VE-04 item that removed the record first.
- **The lane's paths list api/\_lib/abuse.ts.** jobs-api.ts and account-api.ts import `requireTrustedJsonRequest` from it. The lane test now also works out, from the relative imports of api/\_lib/everywhere/\*\* and api/everywhere/\*\*, every file outside those folders that the server code imports, and requires each one in the paths, so the hand-kept list cannot fall behind again.


## 2026-11-11, VE-06 continuation (P1c)

- **Prepared Actions dispatch.** The original port returns a ticket hash only after dispatch, but the job state machine needs that hash before its atomic claim-versus-dispatch transition. A prepared port now returns the hash plus commit/cancel closures. Preparation performs no workflow dispatch. The job reserves `dispatched` first; only its winner commits. An uncertain commit remains dispatched until its start-hook timeout, never offered to another runner. The @vector owner supplies the prepared API; this branch does not edit that owner's files. No Actions adapter is registered in P1c.
- **Terminal posting outbox.** After-response events alone can be lost when a process exits, or when the platform post fails after the terminal state was committed. The same fenced transition now appends the job id to `jobs:posts`. Result events consume that index, and the authenticated sweep retries it. A per-job sixty-second posting lock bounds concurrent delivery, failed deliveries wait a minute, and adapters must update their own job-marked result idempotently. The index contains ids and retry timestamps only, no payload. Account/install erasure removes the relevant pending ids; vanished records are removed by the sweep. Undecodable records remain for a newer deployment. Summary ciphertext is cleared only after a successful post. The existing FakeStore implements the exact added Lua line; the real Redis path remains CI-only.
- **Offer reconciliation.** The twenty-second deadline starts before the relay offer call. Even when the relay reports unavailable, the router rereads the authoritative job: an offer may have reached a computer before its response was lost. Losing a queue or dispatch transition to a claim also returns the laptop outcome. The offer stores a queue deadline only when the actual fallback permits waiting.
- **HTTP acknowledgement.** The platform HTTP response is independent of slow stores. Delivery dedupe and the first platform signal run after it; the signal precedes every routing port. Duplicate deliveries send no second signal. P2 adapters own each platform's exact acknowledgement payload and result idempotency.
- **Production event wiring.** The deployed jobs API is built with all five event hooks, shared with the sweep. P2 supplies configured platform adapters in the runtime; no platform or relay is activated by this package.

Validation receipts and remaining phase gates are recorded in `vector-everywhere-progress.md`.


## 2026-11-11, handoff CI: canonical display-link ownership

The real general CI failure on both platforms was the unlisted pure GitHub display address in core/everywhere/frame.ts. The shared schema already owns import-free platform URL validation. `githubThreadUrl` now owns canonical display-link construction there too, and core framing delegates to it. Valid outputs are unchanged; new cases reject invalid ids and repository/host injection. No address string was obscured and no compliance test or source registry was edited. The unchanged compliance test passes locally; fresh hosted confirmation awaits the phase push.


## 2026-11-11, VE-07: unattended boundaries

- The specified ordinary worktree endpoint is unsafe for unattended use: `Worktree.create` starts project startup scripts. The new main request spells out the required safe capability. The runner refuses its absence; it does not reinterpret a normal endpoint as safe. No avoided engine file was edited.
- `unattendedRules` preserves the user's deny/ask rules for each allowed editing tool, including wildcard permission keys, then explicitly denies dangerous tools. No shell-allow boolean is exposed; an actual R-M2 sandbox must precede shell support.
- The git command flags in the contract do not disable URL rewrites, filters, includes or arbitrary credential helpers. The runner also refuses unrecognized repository config, ignores global/system config and supplies git authentication only through that child's private environment. Its own canonical HTTPS URL and explicit branch refspec never consult a named remote; fetched refs are per job instead of shared FETCH_HEAD. No raw child-process error or authentication value is returned.
- Public or newly public repositories, secret-bearing diffs, posting-check failures/interactive decisions and changed control metadata never authorize pushing. A failed summary check produces only the fixed withheld text. Approval requests are rejected, and any such request prevents partial publishing.
- Persistent in-flight ids/leases use an injected private atomic file adapter. Production file location, signing keys, relay wiring and actual safe-worktree qualification remain VE-21/main-owner work; tests use only generated secrets, loopback servers and disposable repositories.

## 2026-11-12, VE-08: actual listener budget evidence

The engine behaviour check makes real loopback HTTP requests through the desktop client. `Server.listen` builds its own service graph, so AppRuntime's `SpendGuard.snapshot` is not evidence about that listener. Core spend events also have no instance location and are filtered from the instance `/event` stream. The test therefore uses the existing authenticated `/api/session/:id/spend` route to read the listener's retained budget snapshot. This changes the fixture observation only: all lower-budget, priced-cap, no-continuation, personal-memory positive-control, taint and permission assertions remain. The supported raw `permission: "allow"` shorthand is written into the disposable config before its trust confirmation; `Config.Info` itself uses the normalized object shape. No engine implementation changed.

## 2026-11-12, P1c self-review: actual repository and PR identity

The allowed-project declaration alone cannot prove that its current folder is still the configured repository. The hardened git helper now matches an actual local remote with `parseGithubRemote` before the runner creates a worktree or session, and snapshots recheck it. This uses the same parser without calling the ordinary unhardened git-remotes helper. A nonmatch declines `not-mapped` under the claim's exact lease. The push target remains canonical HTTPS for the proved repository, avoiding raw remote credentials and SSH behaviour. Existing marked PR responses now pass the same strict repository URL check as new responses; both require a positive pull-request id and reject extra path, credentials, query and fragment.

## 2026-11-12, P1c independent-review repairs

- **Delivery admission is atomic with the job.** A sixty-second `pending` flag alone could expire after create committed but before `done` was stored. Intake now uses `pending:<jid>`, and the optional delivery key in `CREATE_JOB_SCRIPT` must equal that exact fence before any job write. The same script preserves `job:<jid>` for seven days. Finish is fenced and cannot overwrite an admitted reference or a newer worker. This changes the delivery value, not its key or platform retention; it contains a job id only, with no task, account or credential. A crash after admission leaves the same unrouted job to the existing stale-job sweep rather than creating another one on retry.
- **Retained result pages make progress.** Undecodable/newer records and missing adapters remain queued, but each attempted page rotates those ids one minute forward. `ZADD XX` cannot restore a concurrently erased member. This preserves rollback compatibility while letting later replies proceed.
- **Active worktree config is a separate authority.** Git's `--local` excludes `config.worktree` when `extensions.worktreeConfig` is enabled. Common config is validated first, then hardened `rev-parse --git-path config.worktree` locates the optional file. Only confirmed absence counts as empty; existing files are bounded and explicitly parsed with `--file` and `--no-includes`. This handles a new linked worktree without masking unreadable/malformed/oversized config. Both scopes are checked against the allowlist and rechecked before network/writes. A remote name whose URLs conflict across scopes cannot prove the declared repository.
- **Metadata paths themselves are checked.** A changed `.git` pointer can leave old config hashes unchanged. The canonical working directory, absolute git directory and common directory are now recorded and compared again, alongside the hashes. A `core.worktree` redirect cannot substitute another working directory. These checks do not replace the still-missing safe-worktree creation capability.
- **Three plus signs can be source text.** Only unified-diff file headers outside a hunk are omitted. Added lines inside counted hunks always reach secret/privacy checks, including lines beginning with `++`. Malformed/truncated/unsupported patches fail closed.

The focused local regressions and API/desktop typechecks now pass; independent re-review closed these findings and the optional-file compatibility delta. The final actual-git file passes seven cases / 73 assertions. Root owns the pending normal branch push and exact-head hosted qualification, including the real Redis admission race. Detailed receipts and remaining coverage are recorded in `vector-everywhere-progress.md`; no production-readiness claim follows from these component results.

## 2026-11-12, VE-10: verified references and handoff acceptance

- **Primary sources reread.** GitHub's [OIDC reference](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/security/oidc), [webhook practices](https://docs.github.com/en/webhooks/using-webhooks/best-practices-for-using-webhooks), [issue comments](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/issues/comments), [review comments](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/pulls/comments), [collaborators](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/collaborators/collaborators), [publisher badges](https://docs.github.com/en/apps/github-marketplace/github-marketplace-overview/about-marketplace-badges), and [agent API](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/how-tos/use-copilot-agents/cloud-agent/use-cloud-agent-via-the-api) were read before this package. Review comments remain distinct from conversation comments, author ids/timestamps are available, and collaborator grants are checked instead of display associations. No assignment trigger or new permission was added. @vector's owner confirmed its verifier already accepts both legacy and immutable-ID subject formats; this branch does not modify it.
- **The envelope lacked the proof the desktop needs.** The shared GitHub reference now optionally carries the verified binder's `actorId`, the event family and body SHA-256. Legacy references still decode for compatibility but the new desktop reader refuses them without author/event proof. New fields are reference metadata only; the server still admits no GitHub task or thread text. A handoff's `thread` is constrained to the exact repository/number reference or canonical thread link, and its opaque bounded workflow version must equal the current binding's recorded version.
- **Claim versus acceptance.** Returning handled on claim alone raced the required post-claim `trigger-changed` decline. For GitHub only, the runner reads and validates the thread before its first `started` report. The handoff waits at most twenty seconds for that report; its fenced timeout can refuse received/offered/claimed work but never hands back work whose started transition won. A pre-start heartbeat cannot accept a GitHub job. Already-started work remains handled even if it later fails or is cancelled, preventing an Actions fallback from overlapping it.
- **Persistent status identity.** The received reaction remains @vector's route job. Status comments begin only after verified start; unclaimed/refused handoffs post no refusal. Comment ids are merged into sealed job replies through a fenced internal setter. The optional App port must find only its own exact marker, allowing a retry after an external post succeeded but its reference write failed. A thirty-second lock bounds progress; terminal posts retry through the existing outbox when locked, and stale progress skips a terminal job. Missing App port means no comment, with no fake readiness.
- **Live uncertainty remains a gate.** The real OIDC/current-binding/status ports and workflow route wiring remain absent until VE-31/R-A5/R-A6. An uncertain admitted handler error returns an error, never handled:false. Before live wiring, the workflow must hold on an uncertain/lost handoff response or obtain an authoritative outcome; treating a network failure as handled:false can overlap a committed laptop run. The contract's repeated-run false response is retained and tested, so it must not be used as an idempotent success query. No live handoff is claimed by this candidate.

Root's five focused VE-10 files and API/schema/desktop typechecks pass; exact receipts are in the progress journal. P1c's actual Redis test job `130987536311` passed at `8a6d7b4ef`, including its delivery-admission race. The coordinating agent subsequently confirmed the entire nine-job dedicated lane green. General CI found two real failures repaired locally in `565c03ea8`; fresh hosted confirmation remains owed. These older-head receipts do not qualify VE-10's new acceptance logic. Whole-P2 independent review and fresh CI follow VE-13.


## 2026-11-12, VE-11 candidate boundaries (validation pending)

- **Shared factory, not route registration.** Independent serverless reports and sweeps cannot see a route module's mutable registration. The runtime candidate constructs configured Slack adapters deterministically for each entrypoint. Unconfigured/default-off platforms remain absent; GitHub's real App/OIDC ports are still unbound.
- **Native preparation and controls.** Intake now permits a bounded native membership/context preparation after its first signal, preserving platform/install/delivery/actor/thread/place identity and forbidding shared-to-private widening. The new adapter's account-link/help controls run before requiring an existing link, without creating a job. Native processing is refreshed on heartbeat only for adapters explicitly opting in.
- **Channel privacy is conservative.** The minimum scope set cannot prove whether every channel is private. All channel replies therefore use shared-place output and requester ephemeral summaries. Only verified DMs qualify as private; no added channel-info scope is requested. Grid membership requires the same team or a matching enterprise plus explicit team membership, and foreign/guest/bot requesters are refused.
- **History quota is shared.** One sixty-second workspace key permits one GET `conversations.replies`, limit fifteen and no pagination. Subsequent work omits history and adds an untrusted platform context note. It never promotes teammate/history text into the task. Persistent per-installation/method cooldown keys honor Retry-After across cold starts.
- **No invented Slack idempotency guarantee.** The current `chat.postMessage` method and official argument type do not document `client_msg_id`. Before first post, a sealed attempt receipt is recorded; confirmed message ids are edited on later invocations. Network/5xx/ambiguous native failures retain the receipt and terminal summary instead of creating duplicates. Definitive failures may retry. Unknown first delivery still needs reconciliation and live acceptance before release; it is not silently marked posted. Ephemeral summaries follow the same rule.
- **OAuth retains one account authority.** The signed GET callback only redirects to a fixed same-origin fragment. The existing bearer-authenticated POST consumes the original state before native code exchange. Native grants must match the app, exact bot scope set, installer identity and workspace; unexpected rotation is refused. User/refresh tokens are never stored. A custom injected exchange remains responsible for its own revocation port; the production native factory supplies bot-token revocation.
- **Owner listing steps corrected from current primary sources.** The current Marketplace prerequisites additionally include twenty monthly active users alongside ten active workspaces. Transient encrypted task bodies still require owner review against listed-agent zero-storage rules; this implementation makes no listing/compliance claim. No app was registered. `integrations/slack/README.md` records this and the later surface/prerequisite/live gates.

Primary references reread before the package: [agent development](https://docs.slack.dev/ai/developing-agents/), [agent sessions](https://docs.slack.dev/ai/agent-sessions/), [HTTP events](https://docs.slack.dev/apis/events-api/), [request verification](https://docs.slack.dev/authentication/verifying-requests-from-slack/), [thread replies](https://docs.slack.dev/reference/methods/conversations.replies/), [postMessage](https://docs.slack.dev/reference/methods/chat.postMessage/), [session rename](https://docs.slack.dev/reference/methods/agents.sessions.rename/), [native status](https://docs.slack.dev/reference/methods/agents.sessions.setStatus/), [manifest](https://docs.slack.dev/reference/app-manifest/), Marketplace review and rate-limit references listed in the spec Appendix A. Candidate checks are pending; this entry records design and scope, not successful runtime qualification.


### VE-11 self-review: native authority and erasure (third candidate)

Connect events now select the signed installation home using native authorization/context and matching bot identity, rather than borrowing the foreign event workspace's installation. Explicit org-wide authorizations select only the enterprise record. Native membership must provide boolean identity flags; omissions do not establish a human member. Processing, heartbeat and terminal status now share a fenced lock, with terminal contention retried through the outbox and stale reports checked before further effects. Shared session titles use a fixed name so private repository names cannot appear. Cold factory and actual-route admission regressions were added. Retry-After takes an atomic maximum with ZADD GT and a one-day metadata TTL; no new cooldown Lua was introduced.

A second native lookup race required changing the existing admission script: a stale pre-prepare installation could otherwise outlive erase and its late pass. The coordinator explicitly authorized this precise change. The script compares the exact server-read installation record before quota/job/index writes; native quotas now join that optional transaction instead of being charged separately beforehand. Reconnect and token replacement invalidate the old snapshot. This intentionally refuses a request whose authority changed mid-preparation; a new request uses the new authority. Direct/GitHub creation remains compatible. Deterministic and hosted-only race fixtures accompany the change; validation and fresh hosted Lua proof remain pending for the third candidate.


The installation revision is opaque and fresh on authoritative install/connect, including tokenless same-clock reinstallation. Exact admission snapshots still detect mid-preparation credential replacement. Later native posts compare the authorization revision, allowing a safe credential refresh to preserve its revision while changing the sealed token. This separates credential renewal from a new installation's authority. The generic server writes the proof itself; neither platform text nor the computer chooses it. Pending replies with a missing or changed proof hold for reconciliation. The new final delta is pending focused checks; this is not a live-delivery or Redis qualification claim.

## VE-12 current Linear contract and first validation freeze (2026-11-12)

Re-read all seven Appendix-A Linear pages, OAuth authorization/actor documentation and the SDK schema linked by the official interaction guide. The schema, rather than older illustrative webhook snippets, supplies the activity author and `content.body` fields, app user/organization ids, whole-plan entry shape, native activity UUID input and exact activity lookup. User membership is fetched as the native user with explicit `active`, `app`, `guest` and organization proof; absence never grants authority.

- The current [agent documentation](https://linear.app/developers/agents) requires integration-directory submission to distribute an agent to other workspaces. The spec's unlisted-go-live assumption is therefore an owner distribution gate, not a qualified release path. No app or directory submission was made.
- `AgentSession.creator` is now the responsible human and may be chosen by automation. A plain delegation retains the fixed task line but requests an immutable creator-authored prompt before any job or quota admission. This deliberately holds the unproved human-origin path; editable comments, issue fields and `promptContext` do not establish spend authority.
- `issueRepositorySuggestions` uses Linear inference. This build uses only the team's current mapping and does not invoke it. Exact opaque option values are reconstructed from our own native immutable elicitation and the creator's preceding native prompt, bounded to thirty activities. Missing history, changed mappings, other authors, intervening input and alias text refuse; no model or name fallback chooses a repository. No extra local draft containing native text is stored.
- Authorization-code grants now rotate access/refresh pairs for app actors too. The sealed installation credential bundle stores both together; distributed refresh locking and exact-record CAS preserve the authorization revision and cannot resurrect an erased or replaced install. Native user-link credentials are discarded after viewer proof. The published thirty-minute old-refresh recovery window is a live transport qualification gate.
- `Linear-Delivery` is outside the body HMAC. In addition to validating its native format, the adapter dedupes by signed organization/action/session or immutable activity id. Changing an unsigned header cannot replay a paid request or a previous stop against a later task.
- Native activity creates use a stable supplied v4 UUID. An uncertain response reads that exact object and proves session, app author, content and signal metadata before confirming. It does not invent successful delivery. Token revocation and any definite team-access removal conservatively erase the whole installation; this can require reconnect for teams that remain allowed.
- Generic intake's new optional native-resource reservation is checked as key 12 inside the existing create transaction, after the installation/delivery proof but before quota/job/index writes. Short pending leases cannot overwrite a newer session owner; successful creation extends only the exact reserved owner. Existing callers without this seam retain their path. The new actual-Redis fence fixture is hosted-only and unrun locally.
- All Linear destinations use shared-result rendering. A native response can become an issue comment, so a private summary goes through an injected account/job private-result surface. Until that surface exists, the terminal outbox keeps the sealed summary after posting only the public outcome; no summary is dropped or published on the issue. This is an owner surfaces gate.

The candidate and four focused loopback test files are frozen for the coordinator's first full runtime/package checks. An initial API preflight found a UUID-default inference mismatch; the explicit string nonce parameter repairs it, but fresh receipts are required. No Linear native app, live workspace, real API request, paid inference, new exact-head Redis proof, deployment or production readiness is claimed.


### VE-12 final local qualification

Root batch052 passed all four native files, five shared regressions and API/web types after the second-candidate repairs. The progress journal records exact counts and intentional Redis skips. Native job/auth links now use an absolute HTTPS URL. Refresh rechecks the installation after a delayed token exchange before a later native viewer effect; the client also proves the opened credential pair is still current before using it. A stale token's 401 cannot erase a concurrently replaced pair. Terminal native-owner replacement uses a dedicated atomic CAS plus thirty-second expiry, without changing the permanent identity CAS helper. A lost response therefore cannot leave an unexpiring pending pointer. Deterministic negative/positive cases passed; their new actual Redis scripts remain hosted-only and unproved until fresh P2 CI. This is local software qualification, not native distribution or production readiness.


## VE-13 current Jira contract and first full validation freeze (2026-11-12)

Re-read all seven Appendix-A Atlassian pages and the current Forge Remote/FIT, User/Myself, pre-uninstall and remotes references. The source implements the documented A2A 1.0 `SendMessage` (nested `result.task`), `GetTask` and `CancelTask` (direct Task result), explicit native role/state names, non-streaming endpoint references, user-token API gateway reads and a separate authenticated pre-uninstall endpoint. No Connect or OAuth 3LO fallback is added.

- The current remote-agent and module guides describe direct distribution without an early-access notice. This does not prove product-tier, Free, HIPAA or FedRAMP support. Those remain owner/native gates. No app was registered or deployed. The manifest uses an explicitly unregistered all-zero app-id template.
- Forge Remote currently documents `principal` as UI-only. Every A2A paid/control/poll call therefore requires an actual signed principal; absent identity holds. The body's `userAccountId` must match it. No body, reporter, display-name, Rovo text or user-token-payload fallback establishes a requester. Pre-uninstall permits no person while still proving its exact signed app/endpoint/cloud/installation. Isolated Cloud is unsupported under the pinned commercial JWKS contract.
- `/rest/api/3/myself` requires `read:jira-user`, absent from the spec's three scopes. The coordinator approved this fourth minimum read scope for the source manifest to bind the app-user token to the signed person and explicit active/human/non-guest flags. Missing flags hold. No write, administration or email scope is requested; actual scope consent is still owner work. The manifest also truthfully declares remote storage of in-scope end-user data because temporarily encrypted platform text is stored outside Forge; it makes no residency/badge claim.
- Initial work-item text can include mutable Space Instructions, resource recommendations and Additional Context. It stays untrusted. Assignments use the fixed task line; comment task text requires native author, unedited timestamps and exact body proof. Workflow/manual or changed-comment origins request the person's literal prompt in the existing private context. `AUTOMATION` and initial coding chat cannot spend; Rovo `link <code>` uses the existing one-time private link authority.
- Native context has no immutable prompt-history API equivalent to Linear. Repository/input drafts therefore use a separate encrypted key with a hard TTL of at most fifty minutes, within the two-hour contract. Two-day metadata contains bounded ids, fixed states and sealed rendered output. Exact task/installation/link CAS precedes writes and indexing; installation/account indexes are bounded at 2,000 entries. The static cleanup leaf is imported by erasure bundles, without route registration. Stable ownership-envelope cleanup handles a future business state while preserving a newer authorization's task and advanced context.
- Repository replies must equal a stored opaque option and the current project mapping's immutable repository identity. Names and model guesses never select. Current native user/issue access is rechecked when input resumes. Native message replay recovers the durable task through its atomic message index; concurrent identical deliveries cannot create a second job.
- The response bound is fifty seconds, below the native fifty-five-second budget. The timed path cannot later schedule a paid background job. Queue/run limits remain ten/thirty-five minutes; the fifty-minute response inspects the persisted running/waiting state before lazy sweep changes it and keeps their distinct fixed texts. Terminal native states are persisted, including unconnected control tasks.
- A2A artifacts use shared-result policy because people can publish them as work-item comments. The private account/job summary surface is injected; absence keeps the sealed terminal outbox. System tokens are never used for context. App-user tokens live only inside the current invocation and never enter a task, draft, job, model or log.

Root's first production API preflight passed in 1.26 seconds (batch054). Three semantic repairs and the concurrent-message recovery then changed that source before the first full candidate. All five focused native files, shared regressions and API/web types remain unrun for this latest candidate. New task/draft/erase Lua requires fresh exact-P2-head hosted Redis proof; local fakes and older P1c Redis receipts do not qualify it. The pre-existing shared installation-deletion reconnect race is explicitly queued for the coordinator-approved P2 review repair. No full E13, native, distribution or production readiness is claimed.


### VE-13 checkpoint and open P2 review repairs

The final test-only follow-up passed root batch056 (Jira A2A 19/245 plus web types). All production remains at the locally qualified first-full source; actual new Redis scripts remain unrun locally. The checkpoint does not close E13 or account deletion: independent phase review found stale authorization can cross delayed writes and erasure. A final record deletion CAS alone is insufficient because new/late admissions and content cleanup can race around it. The required repair begins with an exact authorized active-to-closing transition, fences all writers and indexes, binds destructive controls to their generation, and preserves account-closing/tombstone authority. Generic intake also needs the exact person-link proof across its awaited resolver/routing span. Name-only native repository selections must hold or resolve a positive immutable id. These are source repair requirements before the P2 push, not owner actions or waived live-only concerns.


### Whole-P2 authority repair candidate (unqualified)

The independent review's findings are being repaired as one source boundary. Installation removal now begins with an exact active-to-closing CAS before any cleanup. Every cleanup mutation proves that durable closing value. New connects refuse closing and atomically commit their record and connector indexes; a private opaque installation epoch survives record deletion, fencing an absent-to-present-to-absent race. A stale installation/link CAS does not retry over newer authority. Link, repository mapping and OAuth-state record/index updates are atomic. New mappings require positive immutable repository IDs; legacy name-only entries cannot produce native options or admit work.

Generic admission carries the server-read exact link and mapping values through awaited routing into the job-creation transaction before quota/index/body writes. Native jobs seal server-owned install/link revisions; legacy jobs missing those proofs hold. Account writers check both a permanent local Everywhere closure marker and a trusted adapter's exact mode-separated Codium tombstone key inside the same Lua write. The sibling key is read-only and hidden from the original mutation program. This branch has no imported Codium tombstone implementation: a missing adapter is unavailable, and its actual prerequisite merge/wiring remains a gate. Test fixtures explicitly inject generated account authority; production never infers it.

Native controls require a separate policy from ordinary delivery dedupe. Slack event_time is dispatch time, not uninstall chronology; tokens_revoked arrays are user IDs. Signed event receipts pin one captured generation independently of replacement. No-scope auth.test may prove that exact token usable (preserve) or documentably unusable (exact-generation closing); expired/absent/ambiguous errors hold. Linear signed semantic receipts exclude unsigned delivery headers and send timestamps. Renewable access-token failures and team removals cannot prove current-grant revocation, so the source holds without erasure until reconnect or a later qualified native authority contract. The source never guesses a terminal refresh error enumeration.

These are current source changes, not passing evidence. Fresh focused runtime/type checks, full independent re-review and actual new Redis races are still owed before a P2 push. Older VE-11/12/13 and P1c receipts do not qualify this candidate. No platform registration, native request, account service integration, deployment, main change or PR occurred.

The first candidate's independent re-review identified additional concrete repairs before any qualification: resolution now uses one exact mapping snapshot (including initial absence); fixed Lua programs and command batches check every declared Redis key type before their first mutation, because Redis errors do not roll back earlier writes; superseded link-code pointer CAS is enforced; native client guards run after cooldown and refresh waits immediately before platform requests; and Jira hides legacy name-only choices with remapping guidance. The owner installation GET now includes bounded `status: closing` and `nextAction: retry-disconnect` fields for its own interrupted cleanup. GET has no mutation; the existing authenticated DELETE retries using the exact authorized closing snapshot. Automated closing retries remain a scheduled/P4 integration gate rather than an asserted recovery behavior. These changes and their new poststate/race regressions are still unqualified until fresh root runtime/type checks and independent re-review.

The final native review also found that two cleanup callers could let one finish/reconnect while the other's revoker was delayed. Native revocation now records a durable pending phase; only its initiating closer invokes the captured transport, with a final exact-closing `beforeRequest` proof. Another cleanup may erase local contents and credentials but retains an uncertain closing barrier, so reconnect remains refused until the original operation is confirmed or a later qualified native reconciliation resolves it. Only explicit confirmed responses permit final deletion. Unknown/failed outcomes expose `review-native-revocation`; owner retries do not blindly reissue a request or assert a confirmation from an HTTP body. Account deletion can purge local private data without an active-account adapter while retaining minimal native closure metadata. This is not a guarantee about out-of-band native reinstalls or cancellation of provider work already delivered. Process-death and uncertain native reconciliation remain P4/live gates, explicitly unimplemented rather than silently reopening authority.


### Final local repair qualification

Root batches078/079 now qualify the final affected runtime source and API/web types. The progress journal contains exact file counts, intentional actual-Redis skips and repaired earlier failures. Two read-only production reviews closed the storage/deletion/actor boundary and native revocation/owner recovery boundary. The default eraser no longer silently depends on an optional jobs port: it uses the same exact-closing facade for foreign-sponsored cancellation/body erasure. Canonical counter checks reject leading zeros in both `readCounts` and Lua before any earlier quota increment. Native revocation requires an exact original closing proof and an explicit outcome, while uncertain local cleanup scrubs private data and holds minimal closure metadata without requiring active-account authority.

Fresh exact-P2-head hosted Redis/general CI remains owed. Earlier P1c/VE-11/12/13 receipts cannot substitute for the new scripts or final source. No real native registration, credential/settings mutation, paid model, owner computer or deployment was used. P4 process-death/native uncertainty reconciliation and the provider's execution of already delivered work remain real gates; final preflight is not claimed to undo external effects.

## 2026-11-15, EV-01: the engine behaviour test imports the desktop client (F5)

- **Accepted cross-package test.**
  - packages/engine/test/everywhere/unattended-session.test.ts:9 imports `createEverywhereEngine` and `unattendedRules` from `../../../desktop/src/main/everywhere-engine`. It is the only engine test that imports desktop source (runtime-build-config.test.ts only names desktop paths, and the engine source files that mention desktop do so in comments), and engine does not depend on desktop.
  - It stays: VE-08 (spec §8) must drive the exact desktop client the runner uses against the real authenticated loopback listener. A copy, or a re-export through core, would test a different client.
  - Moving the client into `@vectordevai/core/everywhere`, the review's other option, would put desktop runtime code into core for a test's sake.
  - That desktop file imports only `@vectordevai/core` and `@vectordevai/schema`, which engine already depends on, plus a type from everywhere-scan.ts (which imports only `@vectordevai/schema`). So engine's compiler settings check exactly two desktop files.
- **Turbo-cache caveat.**
  - The root `bun typecheck` (`bun turbo typecheck`) is what the pre-push hook and the lane's pre-push rule run. Turbo hashes engine's typecheck from engine's own package files, and desktop is not an engine dependency.
  - So after a desktop-only edit to everywhere-engine.ts or everywhere-scan.ts, turbo can serve engine's earlier pass from cache and hide a break that engine's settings would catch. Desktop's own typecheck still checks both files under desktop's settings.
- **Mitigation.**
  - vector-everywhere.yml runs uncached `bun typecheck` in packages/engine (its `engine` matrix entry, :139-141) on every lane run, and manual runs always run (its scope job). Its picker also runs unattended-session.test.ts on every lane run, because the file sits under `engine/test/everywhere/`.
  - Its pull_request paths include `packages/desktop/src/main/everywhere-*` (:29), so a pull request that edits the client runs both.
  - typecheck.yml's engine job (`bun typecheck` in packages/engine) is also uncached.
  - On everywhere-finish every lane run is a workflow_dispatch until a pull request from the branch is open (then the `pull_request` paths above also start it), so a package that edits either desktop file dispatches vector-everywhere.yml, and runs `bun typecheck` in packages/engine directly as well as the turbo typecheck.

### EV-01 decisions

- **The base moved first.** `release-finish` had moved from `0ee486e3` to `cf2373c4` (13 commits, none touching a lane file or a file the Tier B table cites, and no package.json or bun.lock change). The lane rule says to merge it before a package, so EV-01 starts with merge commit `5733ceea` (`chore(everywhere): merge release-finish into everywhere-finish`, Vector identity and the session trailers). EV-01 is that merge commit plus one docs commit; the turbo typecheck and the CI dispatches run with the push, and their run ids go in the next progress entry.
